            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
     9 & 10 September 2001, the details of which were left on the DAS (LA) Ops & Pol 1
     answerphone. This office is the focal point within the MOD for correspondence relating to
     'UFOs.'

             First, it may be helpful ifi explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
     'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
     defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
     might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
     potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
     to identity the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believ~~s possible that rational
     explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be'~\md for them, but it is not the
     function ofthe MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
      expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

             With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
     files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for either the 9 or I 0
     September 2001 from Yorkshire. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
     suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

        I am writing with reference to your report of several sightings of an 'unidentified flying
object' during September 2001, the details of which were left on the DAS (LA) Ops & Pol I
answerphone. This office is the focal point within the MOD for correspondence relating to
'UFOs.'

         First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observations, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings during the month of
September from Nottinghamshire. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

             I am writing with reference to your report to the Ministry of Defence of a sighting of an
     'unidentified flying object' on 15 September 2001. This office is the focal point within the MOD
     for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

              First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
     'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
     defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
     might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
     potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
     to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
     explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
     function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
     expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

             With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
     files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 15 September 2001
     from anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
     suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity

                         I am writing with reference to your report to the Ministry of Defence of a sighting of an

                  correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                          First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
                  'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
                  defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
                  might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
                  potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
                  to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting_ We believe it is possible that rational
                  explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
                  function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
                  expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit

                          With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
                  files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 9 September 2001
                  from CornwalL We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United
                  Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity

                                                                         Date
                                                                       'Z;j- September 200 I


                I am writing with reference to your report to the Ministry of Defence of a sighting of an
        'unidentified flying object' on 4 August 200 I. This office is the focal point within the MOD for
        correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
        'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
        defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
        might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
        potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
        to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
        explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
        function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
        expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

               I should wish to assure you that the integrity of the UK's airspace in peacetime is
        maintained through continuous policing of the UK Air Policing Area by the Royal Air Force and
        we remain vigilant for any potential military threat. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating
        evidence to suggest that on 4 August 200 I the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by
        unauthorised air activity.

            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    16 September 200 I. The report was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
    (Military) at RAF West Drayton as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
    correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

            First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identity the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
    files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 16 September 200 I
    from anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
    suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

        I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
II September 200!, the details of which were left on the DAS (LA) Ops & Pol !. This office is
the focal point within the MOD for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

        First, it may be helpful if! explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identity the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 11 September 2001
fi·om anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity

                             I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
                    27 August 2001. The report was passed to the Ministry of Defence by Manchester airport and this
                    office is the focal point within the MOD for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                            First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
                    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
                    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
                    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
                    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
                    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
                    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
                    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
                    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                           I should wish to assure you that the integrity of the UK's airspace in peacetime is
                    maintained through continuous policing of the UK Air Policing Area by the Royal Air Force and
                    we remain vigilant for any potential military threat. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating
                    evidence to suggest that on 27 August 2001 the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by
                    unauthorised air activity.

                I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
        11 August 200 I. The report was passed to us by RAF Cosford as this office is the focal point
        within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
        'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
        defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
        might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
        potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
        to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
        explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
        function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
        expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
        files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 11 August 2001
        from anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
        suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

                    Thank you for your recent letter regarding 'unidentified flying objects', which has been
             passed to the Ministry of Defence. This office is the focal point within the MOD for
             correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                     First, it may be helpful ifi explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
             'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
             defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
             might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
             potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
             to identity the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
             explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
             function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
             expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                     With regard to your particular observations, I have looked back through our sighting report
             files and can confirm that we receiveq no reports of'UFO' sightings for 28 January 2001 or for 4
             April 2001 from Kent. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the
             United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

               To   --""~"""-=-':-Y_,_·-~-=(_=---      Ref No           (p~s;f                 12oot

                                                       Date _ _~_2:::.____,_\_'{-\\,....;:'C::)=-c..)

                     The Secretary of State I --::-:---:-:-:---=:--:--has received the
               attached letter from a member of the public. This office has not
               acknowledged it.

                     Please send a reply on behalf of the Minister. All Ministers attach
               importance to such letters being answered promptly; your reply should
               therefore be sent within 20 working days of receipt in this branch. If,
               exceptionally, this should prove impossible an interim reply should be
               sent within the same timescale.

                     A new Open Government Code of Practice on Access to
               Government Information came into force in January 1997. All replies to
               members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set out
               in the Code. A full explanation ofthe Code of Practice is contained in
               DCI(                      information is available from DO Info on


                      Under the Citizens' Charter, Departments are now required to keep
               records of their performance. All branches and Agencies are required to
               keep information on the number of requests for information which
               refer to the Code of Practice including details of the correspondent and
               the nature and date of the reply. In addition, the Department is required
               to provide a record of the total number ofletters from members of the
               public and provide statistics (which may be used on a valid sample) of
               its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

                    As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on
               the accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be
               performed throughout the year.

                      I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
              4 August 2001. The report was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
              (Military) at RAF West Drayton as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
              correspondence relating to 'UFOs'

                       First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
              'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
              defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
              might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
              potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
              to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
              explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
              function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
              expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                      With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
              files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 4 August 200 l from
              anywhere in England. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the
              United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

       potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
       to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational

                I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
        19 August 2001. The report was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
        (Military) at RAF West Drayton. This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
        correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                 First, it may be helpful ifi explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
        'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
        defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
        might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
        potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
        to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
        explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
        function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
        expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
        files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 19 August 200 I
        from anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
        suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

•   REPORT OF AN UNEXPLAINED AERIAL SIGHTING


    I.   Date and time of sighting.
         (Duration of sighting.)

    2.   Description of object.
         (No of objects, size, shape, colour,
         brightness, noise.)


    3.   Exact position of observer.
         Geographical location.
         (Indoors/outdoors,
         stationary/moving.)


    4.   How object was observed.
         (Naked eye, binoculars, other
         optical device, camera or
         camcorder.)

    5.   Direction in which object was
         first seen.
         (A landmark may be more helpful
         than a roughly estimated bearing.)

    6.   Approximate distance.


    7.   Movements and speed.
         (side to side, up or down,
         constant, moving fast, slow)


    8.   Weather conditions during
         observation.
         (cloudy, haze, mist, clear)

                                                                            28 August 2001


                    Thank you for your letter dated 2 August 2001, enclosing a video tape taken of an unusual
            object on 31 December 2000 at 17:00. This office as we are the focal point within the Ministry of
            Defence for correspondence relating to 'unidentified flying objects.'

                    First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
            'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
            defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
            might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
            potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
            to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
            explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
            function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
            expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                    With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
            files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 31 December 2000
            from anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
            suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity. I hereby
            return your video tape.

            Thank you for your letter dated 26 July 2001. This office is the focal point within the
    Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'unidentified flying objects.' I apologise for
    not responding sooner but we have recently moved buildings and this has caused some disruption
    to our normal working practices.

            First, it may be helpful ifi explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identity the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
    files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 23 July 2001 from
    anywhere in the Midlands area. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest
    that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

                                              Thursday 26th July 2001.

    Dear Sir I Madam,

         Although I am not sure whether this is of any direct
    relevance or significance to you, please find enclosed a brief
    description of an unusual aircraft seen on 23/7/01..

         I appreciate that it may be unrealistic to expect such a
    brief siting to be identified. I do feel, however, that it is
    worth reporting. I have not informed anyone else or made any
    further enquiries into it. I am, on the whole, keen to maintain
    my anonymity.

         I thank you in advance for your time and any help in this
    matter.


         Yours faithful

                                                                2 AUG 2001

                                                          Flt::.:E=-:::::::-=::.1
•              Report Concerning Unidentified Aircraft.
    Date. Time:    23/7/2001, c.ll.OO am
    Location:     The back garden at home, on the edge of Perton, 6
                  miles West of Wolverhampton, South Staffordshire
                  (approx. 8 miles S-E of RAF Cosford, 23 miles N-W
                  Birmingham Airport) .
    Weather:      warm and sunny, very clear; blue sky partly
                  covered with (low) slow moving cumulus cloud.
    My Circumstances:
    I was writing while sitting on a bench. I paused to watch a
    high-flying passenger aeroplane low-down on my left,
    travelling roughly in a NW-SE direction. I watched its vapour
    trail and then, after it had lost its vapour trail, continue
    until approximately overhead. I then noticed the second item
    as described below.
    Setting:
    I was immediately taken by this object as it was very
    different to the passenger plane. It appeared much lower as
    it constituted a much larger image size. It was nearly
    overhead when I saw it and, as it was coming towards me, had
    presumably flown from over the house. It appeared to be
    travelling very fast in a roughly North-East South-West
    direction. Unfortunately my siting was very brief, lasting
    perhaps only 3-4 seconds and ending as it disappeared above
    the cloud, but it was otherwise very clear and unobstructed.
    Description:
    My immediate reaction was of a rocket, which then changed to
    something resembling a large 'missile' (the former was
    probably more accurate). It was difficult to see detail
    because of the speed it was going. Its basic appearance was
    of a reasonably long and thin aircraft, of which only the
    body was really visible.
    Initially I noticed the underside which appeared to have two
    outer parallel lines which appeared to protrude (or were
    simply darker), with it being slightly concave in the middle.
    It may also have been darker, possibly circular, at the far
    end. As it passed above me, and I saw it side-on, the sun
    reflected on its surface momentarily - an area of it
    glistened and appeared clearly metallic, but not coloured (it
    appeared dull or greyish overall) . The reflection was not
    along the whole side but just in one small area (in the
    manner of sun on water) .
    It may have been uneven or had markings on its side but it
    was blurred and simply travelling too fast to tell. At no
    time did I notice any shape regarding either its nose or
    tail, or any signs of wings. All I was aware of as it entered
    into the clouds was its rapid speed.
Immediately afterwards I heard the distant 'rumble' and echo
of the passenger jet, but this was not interrupted, magnified
or altered by any other sound. I waited to see the object
emerge from the clouds but did not sight it again. It left no
trail and appeared to make no noticeable noise whatsoever.
It happened so quickly that I doubt whether anyone else
nearby could have spotted it (including my parents who were
working in the garden at the time) .
Height:
This is difficult to estimate as I am not familiar with the
height of clouds. The initial passenger plane, which flew
across a clear part of the sky, was distinctly visible and
not at the very high altitude of some aircraft. I can only
describe the height of the clouds as typical for a warm
summer's day.
My Conclusion:
Initially I thought of something like space-debris, but the
aircraft appeared to be lower than this so this may not be
the correct explanation. It appeared to be travelling in a
line as an aircraft (ie., not up or down).
Myself:
I am not a trained observer, although I look at the sky more
than most. I have long been interested in astronomy and
bird-watching. I have seen many types of aircraft; ie. an
increasing number of passenger planes at home overhead,
sometimes low-flying jets, as well as those from air shows at
a nearby airbase. Unfortunately, I am not particularly
familiar with meteorology. But this was very different to
anything I have seen before.

    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
     explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the

     expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    30 July 2001. The report was passed to this office by RAF Brize Norton as we are the focal point
    within the Ministry ofDefence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

            First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
     expenditure of public ftmds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            , With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
    files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 30 July 2001 from
    anywhere in the Avon area. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that
    the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    18 July 2001. The report was passed to the Ministry of Defence by the Scottish and Oceanic Air
    Traffic Control Centre as this office is the focal point within the MOD for correspondence relating
    to 'UFOs.'

            First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations. such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena. could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our speci!ic defence remit

    files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 18 July 2001 from
    anywhere in Scotland. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the
    United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

          Thank you for your three letters. I apologise for not replying sooner, but we have recently
    moved buildings and this has caused some disruption to our normal working practices.

           Firstly, you asked if we had received any sighting reports of "unidentified flying objects"
    between 25 June - 7 July 2001. The details of sighting reports received within this period are
    given below:

    l;:::;.i;l!!tll . . :I Ti-~lqjt:: .:.:.JII':l:IJ ... r       ' <itt: ••h~litl!iSI!fcip~!Q!!'\I'II'I!;i>    (!;i{i'ili:    I
    . 25106101      21 00 Uannon,                   1 large black disk which changed to an oval shape
                          Llanelli
     28106101       23:59 Eynsham near              One bright yellow/white light, moving northward
                          Whitney

     30107101       23:30 To the west of            1 oval shaped object with rotating lights
                          Bath

     02107101       19 30' London                   One dark, dome shaped object

     04107101       07:00 Kent                      One bright light, moving in a westerly to easterly direction


              You also reported two sightings, one from Liverpool on 7 July 2001 at 17:00 and the other
    seen whilst travelling along the A638 at 0810 on 10 July 2001. After looking back through our
    files, I can confirm that we did not receive any sighting reports from anywhere in the United
    Kingdom for either the 7 or I0 July 200 I. I can also confirm that we did not receive any similar
    reports of "silver ball" objects seen in proximity to aircraft during the month of July 200 I.

            As you will be aware from previous correspondence, the Ministry of Defence examines any
    reports of 'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might
    have some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's
    airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. We are satisfied that
    on each of these occasions there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United
    Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

                I hope this is helpful.

vapour trail back in the sky, just stationary in the sky was what I
can only describe as an orb (like the ones seen in Mexico), we
watched it for about two minutes, when I said to my wife well go
back to our house and get our camcorder, we rushed back, but by
the time id got outside again the object was nowhere to be seen.

2nd Our witness was travelling along the A638 to join M1 at
Junction 40 the date was 10.7.01 the time 8.10am and she made this
report to us:

"As I glanced up I saw a commercial aircraft travelling at altitU:de
in an easterly direction, (traveling away from me.) It was clearly
visible, complete with vapour trial and it was being followed by a
silver ball that I judged to be at the same altitude. I estimated that
the ball was about a quarter ofthe size of the aircraft, following
some six to eight lengths behind it, keeping the same distance. As
the aircraft made a turn of about 20 degrees, the silver ball took a
shallower path in the turn but continued following the aircraft.
At that point, it was my tum to join the traffic at the roundabout,
and as I was joining the M1 I didn't get a chance to pull over to
continue watching this strange sighting. "

                 First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
         'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some

         might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
         potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
         to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
         explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
         function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
         expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        Dear Sir,

        I wonder if you would be kind enough to help me identify a sighting of an unidentified flying
        object that I witnessed with a member of my family tonight?

        Today's date is Sunday S'h July, the time 10.15pm. My location was in North West London,
        facing what I believe to be a westerly direction. I stood looking out of my kitchen window
        when I noticed what I first thought the planet Venus. It puzzled me because it was not in the
        position that I knew it should be, this made me stare a little harder at it. (Note - I used to be
        an amateur astronomer, so I am aware of the positions of planets etc) I watched the object
        for a few minutes before I realized that it was actually moving. I quickly ruled out
        conventional aircraft because it was far to high and there was also no other flashing lights etc.
        There was also no sound. It was very similar to a satellite that flew over London a few months
        ago. The object was exactly like a star and moved very slowly, more slowly that the satellite
        previously mentioned. I also ruled out a comet because I have also seen tihem a few times,
        this was controlled and very near the eartihs upper atmosphere. I watched the object for at
        least 20 minutes before it finally disappeared in cloud cover. I also watched the object through
        a pair of binoculars and it was definitely not a star, I can confirm this by the distance it
        traveled in 20 minutes.

        If you could investigate this for me, I would appreciate it. I checked online at
        http://www.heavens-above.com/ for possible NASA projects, comets or European
        rockets/satellites but none were visible over that section of the sky at that time

        If you need to ask me further questions, please contact me ANYTIME on


        Many thanks for your help

        06){'
                Thank you for your recent letter in which you report sightings of 'unidentified flying
        objects' in the Cumbria area. This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
        correspondence relating to "UFOs".

                First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
        'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
        defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
        might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
        potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
        to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
        explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
        function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
        expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                With regard to your particular observations, I have looked back through our sighting report
        files and can confirm that we received no other reports of'UFO' sightings on either 3 or 20 March
        200 I or 4 June 2001 from the Cumbria area. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating
        evidence to suggest that on these dates the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by
        unauthorised air activity.

        Dear Sir/Madam,

                  I am writing to you about an increase of sightings of so
        called Black Triangles in this area (NW Cumbria). On the 4th of June
        2001 at approx 2pm a large tiangular craft hovered low over a field in
        an area called Moota in Cockermouth, now several witnesses saw this
        craft and it seems to be appearing most nights over this area and is
        causing alarm to the local residence. I was hoping you may have an
        answer to these mysterious crafts or at least picking them up on radar
        as this craft is extremely large and colourful (white/red lights). I also
        have video footage taken on the 3rd and the 20th of March this year
        of a large triangular craft over NW Cumbria.
        I would appreciate if you could shed any light on these matters.
        Yours sincerely

     correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

     'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
     defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
     might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
     potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
     to identity the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
     explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
     function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
     expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond ·our specific defence remit.

            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    I 0 June 2001. The report was passed to this office by RAF Leuchars as we are the focal point
    within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

            First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond pur specific defence remit.

             I should wish to assure you that the integrity of the UK's airspace in peacetime is
    maintained through continuous surveillance of the UK Air Poling Area by the Royal Air Force and
    we remain vigilant for any potential threat. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence
    to suggest that on 10 June 2001 the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air
    activity.

            correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                    First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
            'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
            defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
            might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
            potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
            to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
            explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
            function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
            expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            Thank you for your two recent letters. In your first letter you asked if we had received any
    sighting reports of "unidentified flying objects" within the period 28 April 2001 to 1 May 2001. I
    can confirm that we received one sighting report from the Swansea area on 29 April2001 at 16:20.
    The informant reported seeing three spherical, silver objects travelling from east to west, visible for
    about a minute.

            With regard to your second letter, I can confirm that we received no other reports of
    "UFOs" from anywhere in the UK for 23 May 2001. You also asked if we had received any other
    reports within the period 21 May 200 I to 24 May 200 I. I can confirm that we received two
    reports, both from the Forest Hill area in South East London, on 22 May 2001 at 22:25 and on 23
    May 2001 between 22:05 and 22:40. On both occasions the informant reported seeing a disk
    shaped object with a beam oflight coming from it.

           I hope this is helpful.

    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
    files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 7 May 2001 from
    anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
    suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

    Dear_.

    Thank you for your recent letter in which you have reported three sightings of 'unidentified flying
    objects' between December 2000 and 22 February 2001, all of which you believe are incursions
    of UK airspace.

    As you will be aware from previous correspondence, the integrity of the UK's airspace in
    peacetime is maintained through continuous surveillance of the Air Policing Area by the Royal
    Air Force. Any threat to the Air Policing Area would be handled in the light of the particular
    circumstances at the time and it might if deemed appropriate, involve the scrambling or diversion
    of air defence aircraft.

    With regard to your particular sightings, I can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO'
    sightings in these locations, on the dates you gave. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating
    evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised military
    aircraft.

    In paragraph (b) of your letter, you say that the 'craft' was in 'what you have been assured is an
    air exclusion zone'. Military aircraft do not generally train close to civilian airports and this is
    what may have been meant by an 'air exclusion zone'. However, Woolwich and Greenwich are
    within the Control Zone for London City Airport. Civilian aircraft will therefore be seen in the
    area and will be under the control of London City Airport air traffic controllers. If you have any
    concerns about this air traffic, you may wish to contact the airport direct.

                                   Yours sincerely,

    Dear~

    Sorry I havn't forwarded anything recently, but I've been rather busy, (the recording studio
    rebuild is going very well & we should re-open hopefully in the Summer). Hence enc: is a CD-
    ROM of some of our recent cases we think might be of interest to your dept:

    a) Rendlesham 2000* (actually on the 20th anniversary of the original case) .... This to our
       knowledge is from the only footage available in civilian hands. This object was clearly
       visible for over 20 minutes. We were actually there & witnessed it with at least 30 other
       people inc: one of the original witnesses from Bentwaters (original Rendlesham case) in
       1980 & many well known researchers. We investigated this sighting on the night & have
       made a thorough investigation in collaboration with local research groups to date. In our
       opinion it remains 'unidentified'. It was a clear incursion of the UK's airspace and would
       have posed a danger to legitimate civilian & military aircraft & was in close proximity to a
       former airbase (Bentwaters) esp: as it was at a very low altitude & presumed (to our
       inquiries) to have been below the radar ceiling.
    b) Woolwich Dec'OO* (picture 1). On the 29th Dec'OO a triangular 'UFO' was observed for
       several in minutes over the Millenium Dome in Greenwich. The following photograph was
       taken. This was witnessed by 3 workmen & myself (who took the photo from the studio
       roof). Our research shows this was no kite, balloon, advertising blimp etc ..... the 'craft' was
       under obviously intelligent control. Clearly below 200 feet in altitude and in direct breach of
       what we have been assured is (or now was?) an air exclusion zone. Both in a sensitive area
       & at a sensitive time. It is also very close to the flightpath of the Docklands City Airport. In
       our opinion posed a danger to local air traffic & was thus of UK defence significance.
    c) Woolwich Feb'01 * (picture 2). On the 22nd Feb'01 a bright luminous orange spherical
       'UFO' was observed in almost the same location as the previous sighting. It was witnessed
       by myself and a workman on site. (I was able to take this one photo, I keep a camera with me
       at all times to photograph the development of the studio re-build). This object was witnessed
       for around a minute and exhibited fast 'controlled (?)' movement, also for a short time
       remaining stationary while in close proximity to the Docklands City Airport, then a very fust,
       low trajectory before disappearing into thin air on a clear day with very good visibility. J
       only just managed to catch it on film. This 'UFO' was in my opinion posed a danger to air
       traffic in the vicinity of the Docklands City Airport. The nature of this sighting rules out
       natural phenomena such as bollide meteors & ball lightning. This object remains
       unidentified.

        Thank you for your letter dated 29 April 2001, in which you report a sighting of an
'unidentified flying object' in Wolverhampton on 20 December 2000. Your letter was passed to this
office as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to
'UFOs'_

        First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 20 December from
anywhere in the United Kingdom_ We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

          The Secretary of State, I                      has received the
    attached letter from a member of the public. It has not been
    acknowledged by this office.

          Please send a reply on behalf of the Minister concerned. All
    Ministers attach importance to such letters being answered promptly,
    your reply should therefore be sent within 20 working days of receipt in
    this branch. If, exceptionally, this should prove impossible an interim
    reply should be sent within the same timescale.

                   A new Open Government Code of Practice on Access to
    Government Information came into force in January 1997. All replies to
    members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set out
    in the Code. A full explanation of the Code of Practice is contained in
    ..., ........ '"' ..... ' 223/99 information is available from DOMD on


          Under the Citizens' Charter, Departments are now required to keep
    records of their performance. All branches and Agencies are required to
    keep information on the number of requests for information which
    refer to the Code of Practice including details of the correspondent and
    the nature and date of the reply. In addition, the Department is required
    to provide a record of the total number of letters from members of the
    public and provide statistics (which may be used on a valid sample) of
    its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

         As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on
    the accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be
    performed throughout the year.

            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    13 April 200 I. The report was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
    (Military) at RAF West Drayton as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
    correspondence relating to 'UFOs.' You also left a message on our answerphone.

            First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identity the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

           It mentions in the report that the sighting was recorded on videotape. If you are still
    concerned that what you saw might have represented something of defence significance, then
    please send the tape in to us, although, as I have said, we can not attempt to identifY the precise
    nature of the sighting. The tape would, of course, be posted back to you once viewed.

    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity_ Unless there is evidence of a

                                                                     Your Reference
                                                                     Our Reference
                                                                     D/DAS/64/2 . -
                                                                     Date
                                                                     9May2001


           I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    4 April 200 I. The report was passed to us by Grampian police via the Aeronautical Information
    Service (Military) at RAF West Drayton as this office is the focal point within the Ministry of
    Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

            First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

           I should wish to assure you that the integrity of the UK's airspace in peacetime is
    maintained through continuous policing of the UK Air Defence Region by the Royal Air Force and
    we remain vigilant for any potential military threat. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating
    evidence to suggest that on 4 April 2001 the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by
    unauthorised air activity.

         Dear

         I am writing with reference to your report of an 'unidentified flying object', seen on
         16 February, in the vicinity of Scarborough. This Department is the focal point within the
         Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs' and your letters to RAF Leeming and
         Humberside Police have also been sent to us for reply.

         I will answer your questions in the same order as your letter.

         Could you clarify this sighting?

         As you will be aware from previous correspondence, the Ministry of Defence's interest in reports
         of 'UFOs' is limited to whether there is any evidence that UK airspace has been compromised by
         authorised air activity. Unless there is such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise
         nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as
         aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the
         MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of
         public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

         With regard to your particular sighting, we are satisfied that there is· no corroborating evidence to
         suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft on this
         day.


         Was the military flying exercises?

         The only military flying activities notified to the Ministry of Defence are major exercises and low
         flying training (below 2000 feet), where a specific booking is required. Individual sorties at
         medium or high level and small Squadron exercises are flown depending on individual training
         requirements and it is therefore not possible to say if there were any military aircraft in the area. I
         can confirm, however, that there were no major exercises taking place and there was only one
         aircraft flying at low level. This was a search and rescue Sea King helicopter operating in the
         general area between 17.45 and 18.45.
                    •


•   Have you had any more sightings reported from anywhere else on the night in question?

    We received three other reports for 16 February and these are summarised below.

    Date & Time:          16 February 2001 18:30
    Location:             Ryhall, Lincolnshire
    Sighting:             Two large, bright objects, no particular shape.
    Distance:             2-3 miles
    Movement:             North to South
    Duration of Sighting: 2-3 minutes


    Date & Time:          16 February 2001 18:20
    Location:             Ruskington, Shropshire
    Sighting:             Two white lights
    Distance:             Right above
    Movement:             Heading east
    Duration of Sighting: Not given


    Date& Time:           16 February 2001 21:00
    Location:             Bishops Castle, Shropshire
    Sighting:             One red, yellow and green light, no particular shape.
    Distance:             20-30 miles
    Movement:             Towards Shrewsbury
    Duration of Sighting: 2 minutes.


    I hope this is helpful.

          The Secretary of State, I                      has received the
    attached letter from a member of the public. It has not been
    acknowledged by this office.

          Please send a reply on behalf of the Minister concerned. All
    Ministers attach importance to such letters being answered promptly,
    your reply should therefore be sent within 20 working days of receipt in
    this branch. If, exceptionally, this should prove impossible an interim
    reply should be sent within the same timescale.

          A new Open Government Code of Practice on Access to
    Government Information came into force in January 1997. All replies to
    members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set out
    in the Code. A full explanation of the Code ofPractice is contained in
                              information is available from DOMD on


          Under the Citizens' Charter, Departments are now required to keep
    records of their performance. All branches and Agencies are required to
    keep information on the number of requests for information which
    refer to the Code of Practice including details of the correspondent and
    the nature and date of the reply. In addition, the Department is required
    to provide a record of the total number of letters from members of the
    public and provide statistics (which may be used on a valid sample) of
    its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

         As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on
    the accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be
    performed throughout the year.

        You may recall that I wrote to you on 12 April concerning your report of a sighting of an
        'unidentified flying object' on the evening of28 January and your belief that there were also RAF
        jet aircraft in the area.

        Military aircraft do occasionally conduct training at weekends and the activity you describe could
        have been fighter affiliation training, where one aircraft assumes an attack role and the others
        manoeuvre to avoid it. However, without checking with every fast jet Squadron in the RAF, the
        RN and the United States Air Force, which are based in the UK, it is not possible to be certain
        whether there were any fast jets flying on the evening of the 28 January. Nevertheless, as you
        thought the aircraft you saw may have been Tornado F3s, I have checked with the RAF Stations
        where these aircraft are based and they have confirmed that there were none flying on the date
        you gave.

        I am sorry that I am unable to assist any further with your enquiries.

                                              Yours sincerely,

                                                                   12 April 2001


    Thank you for your letter of 8 March, addressed to my colleague,-in which you
    describe in more detail your sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on the evening of
    28 January and your belief that there were RAF Tornadoes in the vicinity.

    On receipt of your sighting report from RAF Valley, a check was made with air defence experts
    who confirmed that there was no evidence to suggest that UK airspace had been breached by
    unauthorised military air activity on the date you gave and no air defence aircraft had been
    scrambled. However, as you seem to be certain that the aircraft you saw were military fast jets,
    I will make further enquiries and I will write to you again in due course.

                                         Yours sincerely,
•
                                                               08/03/01

           Thank you for your letter of 05/03/0 I. I must admit that I was pleasantly
    surprised to receive a reply regarding my original contact with the RAF.
           In reality I would not expect to have my sighting corroborated by any official
    body, however this does not mean that I did not see what I believed I saw on that
    evening. While I appreciate that it may not be cost effective to further investigate
    this incident, I feel it is worth sending you this, which is my account of the
    incident I witnessed, along with my 15 year old son, if for no other purpose than to
    give you some food for thought.
           At approximately 20.20 hours on the evening of Sunday 28/01/01 I was out
    near my house walking my dogs. I was returning to my house and was walking
    down the grove when a movement in the sky caught my attention. At an
    approximate 45% angle, directly in front of me I could see an object moving left to
    right, at a slow but noticeable velocity.. The object appeared to have an orange
    glow,and I surmised it may be Skylab, which I had not at that time seen. I quickly
    put the dogs inside the house ,called my son, and collected our two pairs of
    binoculars.
           When we got back outside, perhaps 30 seconds had elapsed,. and now the
    object was moving towards us, in a clockwise direction, and was clearly not as far
    away as I had first thought. I estimated the object was at an altitude of between
    three thousand and six thousand feet and traveling at perhaps three hundred knots.
           When viewed through the binoculars the front of the object was glowing
    orange.As the object passed within a mile of us I could see bright orange pieces
    falling from it . My thought at that time was that this was an object that had
    suffered damage, possibly from a collision or possibly from re-entry . Due to the
    circular course it was following it was clearly an object flying under some sort of
    control. Only the front of the object was visible to me as there were no other lights
    showing and I would estimate the size of the visible portion to be perhaps thirty
    yards across. The object disappeared from sight for a period of approximately one
•   to two minutes when it reappeared behind us on what would be the opposite side of
    a circular flightpath .
            It is worth noting that no noise was audible despite its relative closeness to
    us. Just about then we heard aircraft approaching. I turned to see where the noise
    was coming from. Fonn the north east I could make out two aircraft approaching
    and they were following a flight path that would bring them directly over head and
    in the general direction of the object my son was still observing . The planes were
    flying in what I would describe as a tactical fonnation, with one flying slightly
    behind the other and roughly eight hoodred yards apart, and at an altitude of
    between two and five thousand feet.
            Given my basic knowledge of RAF aircraft gained over visiting many air
    shows around the country I have no doubt these aircraft were military jets. I thought
    it to be extremely oousual or unlikely that RAF fast jets would be carrying out an
    exercise over Liverpool on a Sunday night therefore these aircraft may be
    interceptors, making them almost certainly Tornado F3s.
            As they passed overhead although the night was very clear I could not
    honestly say they were F3s for certain ,but they certainly sounded like them. Soon
    however , I was to have a large chunk of doubt removed. The jets now were clearly
    on course to intercept the flightpath of the object which was now roughly five to ten
    miles away,and it's altitude had risen to between five and ten thousand feet.. They
    flew on towards it, picking up speed and height, and it looked as though they might
    bracket the object, but as they approached it, the object suddenly reversed the
    direction it was flying in, away from the approaching aircraft. The two aircraft
    clearly accelerated towards the object, changing direction to pursue it, and it was
    about now that I began to think. What if the thing turns on them and they can't hack
    it, what then? If this was an example of one of these "black" projects, then it was
    obvious that for it to be able to maneuver as it was doing,given the significant
    damage it had sustained, it was employing a kind of technology I had never heard
    of, and it was a very capable craft. If it was terrestrial in origin,then why would it
    fly away as it was doing , and then I thought, if it is terrestrial or otherwise, is it
    flying away to avoid more damage i.e. the damage had been inflicted by the
    interceptors earlier ,and they had caught up with it as it was waiting for some kind
    of backup or rescue ..I began to think about rules of engagement in such
    circumstances, and also about the vehicles we send into space,and do we send them
    up with any kind of defensive armament?
            Anyway, the three craft flew a very short way in line before the object simply
    shot upwards, and the jets also climbed vertically in pursuit. They were lost to sight
    then as they all went up through one of the very few clouds visible in the sky that
    night. By the speed the jets climbed I would say they almost certainly must have
    used afterbumers,as I have seen Hawks, Tornadoes , FI5s, SU27s etc all going
•   vertical, and the Hawks do not climb at such a rate as this pair were doing.
    Afterwards the heavy kerosene smell you get at displays was noticed by my son and
    I, which may seem unlikely, but there was very little wind that night Perhaps
    thirty or forty seconds elapsed when to my reliefl saw one of the jets coming back
    down with the second following soon after.
            The jets flew a couple of circuits of the area taking perhaps another five
    minutes, before they departed in the general direction they had frrst appeared from.
    As they flew off I could hear another, larger aircraft approaching. My first
    impression was that it may be a commercial flight as our house is directly under one
    of the main flight paths into Liverpool airport, Before it flew over us however it
    banked sharply and began a tight circuit and my belief is that this aircraft was a
    Nimrod engaging in a search for the object using its electronic detection systems.
    The silhouette of the aircraft and the noise of its engines and the position of its
    engines would tell me it was a Nimrod. The aircraft continued to circuit the area for
    some fifteen to twenty minutes before it flew towards us , with all its forward lights
    on . As it approached our position it turned all of these lights off leaving only its
    running lights visible . It then began to gain altitude gently and continued in straight
    flight in south westerly direction, until it went out of sight.
            I have already obtained feedback from the CAA regarding air traffic that
    evening and all they could confirm was that two Ryanair Boeings had been in the
    area over Liverpool that evening.
            I do not expect you to confirm or deny any of these facts but I would ask that
    you give them some serious thought.
            Whether this object is of terrestrial or extra terrestrial origin one thing is for
    certain. We as nation or we as a race had better get our fmgers out as we are
    definitely second best to who ever had built this craft.
                         Yours Sincerely

                Thank you for your letter dated 17 April 2001 in which you report a sighting of an
         'unidentified flying object' in Grimsby on 16 April 2001. This office is the focal point within the
         Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs'.

                 First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of

    I am writing with reference to your report of an 'unidentified flying object', seen on 3 April, the
    details of which were passed to us by RAF West Drayton. This office is the focal point within the
    Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs'

    Firstly, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

    Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external military
    source, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the
    precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such
    as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the
    MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of
    public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

    With regard to your particular observation, I can confirm that we received no other reports of
    'UFO' sightings for 3 April from anywhere in the UK. We are satisfied that there is no
    corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by
    unauthorised military aircraft.

                                   Yours sincerely,
